# Boots Online Doctor (UK) — digest
Slug: boots-online-doctor · Verified: yes · Audiences: men

Model: High-street pharmacy's online doctor: a men-only questionnaire for licensed finasteride or Propecia tablets (optionally bundled with Regaine foam), reviewed by a clinician, sold as one-off packs with store pick-up or free delivery.
Account wall: After the health questions. Nothing asks for an email before that: after postcode, birth gender and date of birth the site itself creates a placeholder "temporary user" (POST users/temp). The first screen we could not pass is the ID photo step at /camera/id ("Let's confirm it's you": a selfie and a photo of your ID), which comes straight after the last health question. Account set-up (email, name, phone, address, password) is the next task after the photos, then delivery and payment. No recommendation is shown before the wall: the patient has already picked the medicine.
Tested by: live-walk, site-code, public-docs
Coverage: Walked live with the baseline man from the entry page to the ID photo screen for the first-time, tablets-only route (all 19 question screens). The three other routes (first-time bundle, returning tablets, returning bundle) were walked to their first health question, which made the site serve their full question sets; those questions are recorded from the served definitions, not clicked through. All four definitions are saved. No account, email, name, phone, address, password, photo or payment was entered. A postcode had to be typed to start: the placeholder postcode found in the site's own code (N1 0QH) was used; it is a location check, not a contact detail. There is no women's hair-loss consultation to walk. Stage 2: 14 live one-change runs (x00 to x13, runs/x*) confirmed the gates, the product list limits and that no answer changes the product, price or flow on screen. Stage 3 (independent check): 14 more live runs (runs/verify-*) replayed all four baselines, the gates (plus age 18 and Guernsey), the out-of-stock tile, completed the bundle age-50 run, walked the returning bundle route end to end for the first time, tested one amber and one red answer on their own and the Propecia bundle. It found that the order JSON does record a triage label (answer_status), which stage 2 had missed.

Questions: 192 (180 before sign-in; 66 branch). Flows: men-first-tablets (46q), men-first-bundle (49q), men-returning-tablets (47q), men-returning-bundle (50q)

## Engine summary
There is no recommending engine on screen; the website is a gate, a price list and a fixed form. Three hard stops come first and were all seen live: postcode (Jersey and Guernsey refused), birth sex (female refused) and age (under 18 refused; 18 and 75 accepted with no change). The man then says first-time or returning (no proof) and picks medicine, brand and pack himself. First-timers can only pick one-month packs (bigger packs are marked out of stock in the served product list and cannot be clicked); "returning" unlocks all packs. Tablets or bundle, first-time or returning, picks one of four fixed questionnaires; Propecia uses the same one as generic. Follow-ups appear one level deep. Every option carries a plain/amber/red status. The screen ignores it: even nine stacked red flags and three "I will stop my consultation" answers reach the same ID photo screen with the same product and price. But the server does use it: once the answers are posted, the order is labelled "Normal", "Query", "Warning" or "Danger" for the clinician. Asking to drop Regaine does not change the order. All decisions are left to a clinician after ID photos, account and payment.

## Rules
| id | when | then | effect | evidence | conf |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | Birth gender = "Female" | Hard stop on the second screen: "We're sorry, this service isn't available for the sex you were assigned at birth. Visit Boots Health Hub for advice." There is no women's hair-loss service. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | hard-stop | observed | high |
| R2 | Date of birth gives an age under 18 | Hard stop: "Based on your age, you're not suitable for our online service. Please speak with a local GP or clinic". A man who turned 18 the day before is accepted (runs/verify-r-age18). [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | hard-stop | observed | high |
| R9 | Date of birth gives an older age (75 tested) | No change: same products, prices and stock flags; Regaine bundles are still offered. Only the bundle questionnaire later asks an age band and warns about off-label Regaine use at 50 or over. | none | observed | high |
| R10 | Postcode in Jersey (JE…) or Guernsey (GY…) | Hard stop on the postcode screen: "We do not currently provide our service to this postcode." [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | hard-stop | observed | high |
| R3 | Product chosen is tablets only vs a Regaine bundle | Tablets only loads questionnaire 2049 (35 questions); a Regaine bundle loads questionnaire 2050 (38 questions) with age-band, scalp-skin and blood-pressure questions. [Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | extra-questions | observed | high |
| R4 | "Have you used this service before?" = "Returning for this service" | Child service HAI-R: every pack size is available and questionnaires 2051 (tablets) / 2052 (bundle) are used; they add "…depression, suicidal thoughts and sexual dysfunction can occur. Do you think any of these have happened to you?" for men already on finasteride. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | extra-questions | observed | high |
| R8 | All health questions answered (any answers) | Answers are posted once, at the end. The Health Questions task becomes status 3 and the order admin_status becomes 4.1; the next task is ID Photos, then Account-Setup, Delivery, Payment. No recommendation or result screen is shown. | next-question | observed | high |
| R5 | "Have you used this service before?" = "First time using this service" | Only one-month packs can be chosen (Finasteride 28 £28, Propecia 28 £55, and the one-month Regaine bundles £50.36 and £72). The served product list marks every larger first-time pack out_of_stock: true, the tile says "Out of stock" and clicking it does nothing. Saying "Returning" (no proof asked) unlocks every pack, e.g. 84 tablets for £80. Whether this is real stock or a deliberate first-supply limit is inferred, not known. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | product-removed | observed | medium |
| R11 | Medicine chosen = Propecia instead of generic finasteride | Same questionnaire (2049 for first-time tablets); only the order product and price change (sku 29-9, £55 vs sku 25-9, £28). [Propecia 1mg tablets (brand)] | none | observed | high |
| R6 | Any red-flag answer (status 3 in the definition), e.g. circular patches, complete baldness, painful or pus-filled scalp, "I will stop" answers, treatment not effective, partner trying to conceive, suicidal thoughts, galactose intolerance (tested alone in verify-r-galactose) | The website does not stop the patient: the follow-up warning is shown, Next still works and the flow ends at the ID photo step with the same product and price in the draft order. But the server does act: after the answers are posted the order line field answer_status changes from "Normal" to "Danger" (x08 also set answer_score_1 and answer_score_2 to 2; their meaning is unknown). So red flags are triaged for the clinician, not shown to the patient. Refusal, if any, happens after ID photos, account and payment details. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | flag-for-clinician | observed | high |
| R7 | Bundle: taking blood-pressure medicine = "Yes" → "Yes - please change my prescription to tablets alone." | The website keeps the bundle in the order (sku 817-9, £50.36). The request to drop Regaine is only an answer for the clinician; no price or product change is shown. Server side the order line answer_status becomes "Warning". [Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | flag-for-clinician | observed | high |
| R12 | Bundle: age band = "50 years or older" | An off-label warning for Regaine (licensed up to 49) appears with the choice to continue or switch to tablets only. Choosing tablets only does not change the draft order (still sku 817-9, £50.36); the flow reaches ID photos and the server sets answer_status "Warning". [Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | flag-for-clinician | observed | high |
| R13 | Bundle: blood-pressure medicine = "Yes" → "No" (keeps Regaine), or "High blood pressure" ticked as a condition, or broken/inflamed scalp skin = "Yes" | Red flag (status 3) in the definition; by R6 the website would carry on. Not replayed one by one. [Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | flag-for-clinician | code | medium |
| R14 | Every follow-up question in all four definitions | Shown only when its display_condition lists an option ticked on the first question of the same screen (one level deep). No answer skips a later screen; the number of screens is fixed (19 for tablets, 21 for bundles). | extra-questions | code | high |
| R15 | Health answers posted at the end of any route | The server scores the answers and stores a triage label on the order line (answer_status): "Normal" for the baselines, "Query" or "Warning" when an amber (status 2) answer is given, "Danger" when a red (status 3) answer is given. This is the only place the answers act before a clinician sees them; the screen, product and price do not change. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | flag-for-clinician | observed | high |
| R16 | Medicine chosen = Propecia + Regaine bundle instead of the generic finasteride bundle | Same bundle questionnaire 2050; only the order product and price change (sku 825-9, £72 vs sku 817-9, £50.36). [Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle)] | none | observed | high |

## Who gets what
- Baseline man, 32, temples and crown, gradual, no medical issues, first time → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] No recommendation screen. He sees a price list and picks; only the 28-tablet packs (£28 generic, £55 Propecia) and the one-month bundles (£50.36, £72) can be chosen. The £28 pick reaches the ID photo step with no message. (runs/verify-b01-baseline)
- Same man choosing Propecia → Propecia 1mg tablets (brand) [observed] Same questionnaire as generic; £55 for 28 tablets. (runs/x06-propecia)
- Same man choosing the finasteride + Regaine bundle → Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] Longer questionnaire (2050) with age band, scalp skin and blood-pressure questions; £50.36 for one month. (runs/x09-bundle-baseline)
- Bundle patient on blood-pressure medicine who asks for tablets alone → Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] The order stays the bundle at £50.36 on screen; the clinician is expected to change it to tablets only (inferred). (runs/x10-bundle-bp-drop-regaine)
- Bundle patient aged 50 or over → Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] Warned that Regaine is off-label over 49 and offered tablets only; choosing tablets only leaves the bundle in the order on screen, but the server labels the answers "Warning" (runs/verify-r-x11-age50).
- Man saying he is returning (no proof asked) → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] All pack sizes open, e.g. Finasteride 84 tablets £80; returning questionnaire 2051 adds a side-effect question for current users. (runs/x12-returning-baseline, runs/verify-b04-returning-bundle)
- Man with red-flag answers (patchy or complete loss, painful scalp, sudden onset, partner trying to conceive, suicidal thoughts) → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic) [observed] Nothing changes on screen: the chosen product stays in the order and he goes on to ID photos. The server labels the order "Danger" for the clinician (observed). Refusal can only come from the clinician later (inferred). (runs/x08-all-red-flags, runs/verify-r-galactose)
- Man aged 75 → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (brand); Finasteride 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle); Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] Accepted; same list and prices as the baseline man. (runs/x04-age75)
- Baseline woman, 38 → nothing [observed] Refused at the birth-gender screen; signposted to Boots Health Hub. No women's hair-loss service. (runs/x02-female, runs/verify-r-x02)
- Under 18 → nothing [observed] Refused at the date-of-birth screen; told to see a GP or clinic. (runs/x03-age17, runs/verify-r-x03)
- Jersey or Guernsey postcode → nothing [observed] Refused at the postcode screen. (runs/x05-jersey, runs/verify-r-x05, runs/verify-r-guernsey)
- Same man choosing the Propecia + Regaine bundle → Propecia 1mg tablets + Regaine foam extra strength (bundle) [observed] Same bundle questionnaire (2050) as the generic bundle; £72 for one month (runs/verify-r-propecia-bundle).

## Strengths
- Trusted pharmacy brand, CQC-registered provider, named clinical lead, pick-up in any Boots store within about 2 business hours of approval.
- Licensed medicines only; clear safety wording (pregnancy, PSA tests, depression and sexual side effects) repeated as must-acknowledge screens.
- No email needed until after the questions; pay only if approved; no subscription lock-in.
- Bundle questionnaire handles Regaine limits (age 49 licence limit, blood pressure, scalp skin).
- Every gate it does have is immediate and plainly worded (sex, age, postcode), with signposting to a GP or Boots Health Hub.
- Safety follow-ups appear exactly when relevant (pregnancy, suicidal thoughts, blood pressure with Regaine, off-label Regaine over 49), so the clinician gets context in the patient's own answers.

## Weaknesses
- Men only; nothing for women's hair loss.
- Very narrow range: finasteride 1 mg tablets with or without Regaine foam. No topical finasteride, oral minoxidil, dutasteride, compounded sprays, shampoos or supplements.
- The patient must choose medicine and pack before answering anything; no guidance or recommendation.
- No photos of the scalp are asked for; pattern is one four-option question with wording errors ("Tick all that apply" on a single-choice question).
- Red-flag answers do not stop the flow on screen; the patient can go on to ID photos and payment details and only then be refused.
- First-time list showed most packs as "Out of stock"; ID photo step adds friction; dated copy (pages last reviewed 2021 and 2023, placeholder text left in the content data).
- Live tests show the screen never acts on its own red flags: patients who say they will stop, who have patchy or complete hair loss, or suicidal thoughts still reach ID photos, account and payment steps. The server only labels the order ("Danger") for later review.
- Choosing "tablets alone" in the bundle form does not change the basket; the patient still sees the bundle price.
- "Returning" is self-declared with no check, and it unlocks bigger packs that first-timers cannot buy.
- No questions about goals, how much hair loss bothers the patient, preferences (tablet vs topical) or previous non-finasteride treatments; nothing about women, so half of hair-loss patients are turned away.

## Opportunities
- A guided recommendation (pattern, goals, preferences) instead of a price list.
- Options for men who cannot or will not take oral finasteride, and a women's pathway.
- Immediate, kind on-screen outcomes for red-flag answers with signposting, instead of late refusal.
- Scalp photo review and progress tracking; subscription convenience with easy pause.
- Act on red flags at once with a clear "this is not right for you, here is what to do" screen, saving the patient ID photos and sign-up.
- Let the questionnaire change the basket live (e.g. drop Regaine for blood-pressure patients, suggest topical options for men who do not want oral finasteride).
- Offer longer first supplies or a subscription after a proper first review, instead of stock flags.
- Serve women and the "no finasteride" group (topical finasteride, oral or topical minoxidil, combination sprays), which Boots does not sell online.

## Verification verdict
The core picture holds up: Boots Online Doctor has no on-screen recommending engine, men only, three entry gates (sex, age 18+, mainland UK postcode), the patient picks the product, and that pick chooses one of four fixed questionnaires that are saved word for word. Prices and products were all re-checked live and match. One important claim was wrong: answers are not ignored. The server labels each order Normal, Query, Warning or Danger from the option statuses, which is how red flags reach the clinician. With that corrected, the report can be trusted as labelled for everything before the ID photo wall; nothing after the wall (clinician decisions, refusals, switches) is known.

## Limitations
- The site never shows option status. Stage 3 saw that the server turns it into an order label (answer_status Normal / Query / Warning / Danger), but how the clinician uses that label, and what answer_score_1 and answer_score_2 mean, cannot be seen without an account.
- Nothing after the health questions was seen live (ID photos, account, delivery, payment, clinician messages, refusals). Those steps are described from the order task list and app parameters only.
- Stock flags and prices are as served on 2026-11-23 and may change. The Regaine strength is not stated on the site.
- A postcode had to be entered to begin (the site's own placeholder N1 0QH was used). If the project owner counts a postcode as an address, treat every live walk here as outside the rules and rely on the served definitions only.
- Stage 2 live runs: 14 (x00 to x13). Fully replayed: first-time tablets and bundle baselines, returning tablets baseline, partner trying to conceive, patchy loss, a stacked red-flag run, bundle + blood-pressure medicine, out-of-stock tile, sex, age 17 and 75, Jersey postcode, Propecia questionnaire id. x11 (bundle, 50 or over) was partial; stage 3 completed it (runs/verify-r-x11-age50).
- x08 changed many answers at once on purpose (to test whether any red flag acts on screen). Because nothing changed, each flag in it is shown not to act; but a single-answer effect that cancels another cannot be ruled out completely.
- Not tested live: other conditions one by one (cancer, liver, depression, sexual dysfunction, skin, kidney); prostate medicine; other medicines and allergies (they need free text); scalp-condition ticks; the understanding check "False"; "I'd like to ask more about side effects"; GP "Yes" (opens a GP practice search: third-party details, avoided). By the served code these add a follow-up and an amber or red status, which by R15 should only change the server label, not the order.
- Whether "Out of stock" is a deliberate first-supply limit or real stock is inferred; the flags may change from day to day.
- What the clinician does with flagged answers, and whether the bundle is switched to tablets only, happens after the account wall and was not seen.

# Dr Fox (Doctor Fox) (UK) — digest
Slug: dr-fox · Verified: yes · Audiences: men

Model: Low-cost UK online doctor and pharmacy: a one-page yes/no medical form, the patient picks the product, pay per order with no subscription, a doctor approves before despatch.
Account wall: After the medical form, the 'Choose treatment' page and the 'Confirm order' basket page: pressing 'Confirm order' ('Next: Register/login and pay') opens https://www.doctorfox.co.uk/order/login ('Please login to continue. Create account or login'). The patient sees the full product list and the total price (for example £14.90 for 28 finasteride tablets) before the wall.
Tested by: live-walk, site-code, public-docs
Coverage: The whole medical form (17 questions on one page) is public HTML and was saved and parsed. Stage 2 replayed every question that can change the offer, on both product routes: all 11 stop answers one at a time, the three product-removing answers (Q742, Q743, Q746) on both routes, Q747, Q753 with and without text, one combination, and four baskets to the confirm page to read prices and fees. All three baselines still work (23 Nov 2026). Nothing behind the login page was tested. There is no women’s flow. Stage 3 (independent check, 23 Nov 2026) replayed the three baselines and nine rule tests (all confirmed), re-read the form, order and site code, re-checked all 20 pack prices and the emc licences, and added three new tests: Q743 + Q746, and two runs showing that a second consultation can add finasteride to a basket that already holds the spray.

Questions: 23 (19 before sign-in; 7 branch). Flows: men (23q)

## Engine summary
Dr Fox has no recommendation engine: it is a one-page safety filter in front of a shop shelf. All 17 yes/no questions sit on one public page. Eleven answers are hard stops checked in the browser: the form will not submit, and most messages say the patient can change the answer and continue. One question (Q745, "Do you wish to order the spray?") picks the shelf: the compounded spray alone, or finasteride tablets plus minoxidil products. After submission the server removes products: a finasteride exclusion (Q742 or Q743) takes off generic finasteride, Propecia and the spray; low blood pressure symptoms (Q746) take off Sons, Regaine and the spray. If nothing is left the page says "please cancel this consultation" rather than offering the other family. Heart or blood pressure disease (Q747) only shows a GP message. The patient then ticks up to four packs; nothing is suggested and duplicates (finasteride with Propecia) are allowed. The full price shows before the login wall; a doctor reviews every order after payment. Every rule works per consultation: filling in the form twice can put the spray and finasteride tablets in one basket, even with conflicting answers, and nothing warns before the wall (seen to the confirm page only).

## Rules
| id | when | then | effect | evidence | conf |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|

Finasteride tablets, Propecia, Sons and Regaine are not shown. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride); Dr Fox Dual Action Hair Growth Spray; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5%; Sons Minoxidil 5% solution] | product-switched | observed | high |
| R2 | men-q7 (Q745) = No | The choose page offers generic finasteride and Propecia (4 packs each) and Sons, Regaine solution and Regaine foam (3 packs each), as 17 checkboxes; no spray. The patient picks; nothing is pre-selected. [Dr Fox Dual Action Hair Growth Spray] | product-removed | observed | high |

Most messages say 'You can change your answer and continue'. The check is in the page script (Consultation.checkSubmit); whether the server also refuses these answers was not tested. The form message points to a specialist hair loss clinic for finasteride. [Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride)] | product-removed | observed | high |

No reason given on the choose page. [Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5%; Sons Minoxidil 5% solution] | product-removed | observed | high |
| R9 | men-q8 (Q746) = Yes, spray route | Nothing is offered; 'please cancel this consultation'. Finasteride tablets are not offered instead. [Dr Fox Dual Action Hair Growth Spray] | hard-stop | observed | high |
| R10 | men-q9 (Q747: high blood pressure, heart disease, abnormal rhythm, angina, circulatory disorders) = Yes | Message only ('Please consult your GP before using ... minoxidil'). ## Who gets what
- Baseline man (pattern loss, no conditions, no medicines), declines the spray (Q745 No) → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride); Sons Minoxidil 5% solution; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5% [observed] Up to 4 items. Cheapest: finasteride 28 tablets £10.00 + £2.00 fee + £2.90 delivery = £14.90. Finasteride 28 + Sons 60 ml = £34.85.
- Baseline man, wants the spray (Q745 Yes) → Dr Fox Dual Action Hair Growth Spray [observed] 1 x 60 ml (2 months) £26.00 + £2.90 delivery = £28.90, no prescription fee. 2 packs £48.50, 3 packs £72.00.
- Man on dutasteride or finasteride 5 mg, allergic to finasteride (Q742 Yes), or with a finasteride caution (Q743 Yes), tablets route → Sons Minoxidil 5% solution; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5% [observed] Minoxidil only. Cheapest: Sons 60 ml £19.95 + £2.90 = £22.85 (no fee). No reason shown on the choose page.
- Same man (Q742 or Q743 Yes) who chose the spray route → nothing [observed] Nothing offered: 'please cancel this consultation'. He must go back, answer Q745 No and take the tablets route to be offered minoxidil.
- Man with low blood pressure symptoms (Q746 Yes), tablets route → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride) [observed] Finasteride only (generic or Propecia).
- Man with low blood pressure symptoms (Q746 Yes), spray route → nothing [observed] Nothing offered; told to cancel. Finasteride tablets are only offered if he goes back and answers Q745 No.
- Man with both a finasteride exclusion (Q742 or Q743) and low blood pressure symptoms (Q746) → nothing [observed] Nothing offered; told to cancel.
- Man with high blood pressure, heart disease or a circulation problem (Q747 Yes) → Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride); Sons Minoxidil 5% solution; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5% [observed] Same as baseline on both routes (spray route: p3 only). The only change is a "consult your GP" message about minoxidil.
- Any man giving one of the 11 stop answers (patchy loss or scalp problem, rapid/total/secondary loss, not aware or not agreeing to a warning, declaration No) → nothing [observed] The form will not submit; most messages say the answer can be changed to continue.
- Woman (or anyone assigned female at birth, including trans men) → nothing [docs] Not served for hair loss at all; pointed to their GP and high-street Regaine for Women.
- Man who fills in the form twice: spray route first, then tablets route → Dr Fox Dual Action Hair Growth Spray; Finasteride 1mg tablets (generic); Propecia 1mg tablets (branded finasteride); Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Solution 5%; Regaine for Men Extra Strength Scalp Foam 5%; Sons Minoxidil 5% solution [observed] Both consultations go into one basket (spray + finasteride 28 = £43.80 with two delivery charges) with no warning, even when the second form reports low blood pressure symptoms (minoxidil then drops from the second list only). Seen to the confirm page only (runs/verify-gap-two-consults, verify-gap-t

## Strengths
- Prices at or near the floor of the UK market: finasteride £14.90 delivered for 28 tablets (live walk) and the triple-active compounded spray £28.90 delivered for two months, the cheapest spray per month in the UK market sweep (market/uk-sweep.md); no subscription. The same sweep lists five sellers with a lower headline price for finasteride tablets (£6.95 to £9.99 before their own fees and delivery), so "cheapest" is true for the spray but not proven for tablets.
- The full total, including fee and delivery, is shown before any sign-in.
- Clear, specific safety screens that really act: Q742/Q743 remove every finasteride product and Q746 removes every minoxidil product, server-side (observed).
- Very open about unlicensed and off-label supply, with explicit consent and plain warnings (PSA, mood, sexual effects, breast changes, pregnancy handling, 12-month stop rule).
- Fast and simple: one page, about 2 minutes by our estimate, no photos, no account until the basket.

## Weaknesses
- The hard stops are self-correcting: most messages say "You can change your answer and continue", so the form teaches a patient which answer passes.
- No diagnosis step: no age, pattern, Norwood stage, duration or photos. The patient simply declares male pattern hair loss.
- When products are removed on the tablets route, the choose page gives no reason; on the spray route the patient is told to cancel instead of being offered the products that are still safe for them.
- No duplicate-treatment check before the account wall: generic finasteride and Propecia, or Sons and Regaine, can sit in one basket (runs/exp-23).
- Heart disease, high blood pressure and arrhythmia (Q747) do not change the offer of minoxidil; only a GP message shows.
- Q743 lumps five different cautions into one yes/no, and any Yes means "no finasteride from us" rather than a doctor review.
- Men only: no treatment for women at all, not even topical minoxidil.
- Small errors: spray 2 x 60 ml is £48.00 on the product page but £48.50 at checkout; the fee table says "up to £10 = £1" but £10 is charged £2; the message "You should can not take"; a placeholder "spray only message" shows to patients.
- Some published exclusions have no question: difficulty passing urine (finasteride) and fast loss within the last 12 months.
- No oral minoxidil, dutasteride or stronger options, so no step-up for men who do not respond.
- The "no spray with tablets or minoxidil" rule and every exclusion apply only within one consultation: a second consultation can put the spray and finasteride in one basket, even with conflicting answers (low blood pressure symptoms in the second form), with no warning before the account wall (runs/verify-gap-two-consults, verify-gap-two-consults-conflict).
- No question on allergy to minoxidil or tretinoin, although the spray page lists both as reasons not to use it.

## Opportunities
- Women's hair loss: Dr Fox, one of the biggest UK online pharmacies, refuses it outright and sends women to the high street.
- A real step-up path (oral minoxidil, topical finasteride for men who stop tablets because of side effects, dutasteride referral) for men Dr Fox tells to stop at 12 months or turns away after Q743.
- A consultation that explains itself: say why a product is not offered and offer the safe alternative in the same step, instead of "please cancel".
- Diagnosis and progress: photos and a pattern check at the start and at 6 and 12 months, which Dr Fox does not do.
- Price matching is hard (finasteride £14.90 delivered); compete on care, follow-up and women, not on price.

## Verification verdict
This report can be trusted as labelled for everything before the login page. All three baselines and every rule that changes the offer were re-run or re-read and none was contradicted; all 20 pack prices and the licence facts match the live pages and emc. The one real error was the claim that the spray can never be sold with finasteride: within one consultation that is true, but a second consultation puts both in one basket without any warning, even with conflicting answers. That is now recorded as R23 and R24. Two evidence labels were too strong and one strength overstated the price position; both are fixed. What happens after the account wall (identity, age, the doctor's review, whether duplicates or conflicts are caught) remains unknown and is labelled so.

## Limitations
- Nothing after the account wall was seen: identity checks, the date-of-birth check, the doctor’s review and the final invoice are described only from the site’s own words.
- Whether the server also refuses the 11 stop answers (if the browser check were bypassed) was not tested: that would mean submitting the form outside the normal page flow.
- Whether the doctor removes duplicate products (finasteride with Propecia) or acts on Q747 and the free text after payment is unknown.
- Age and sex experiments could not be run: the form asks neither before the wall.
- Combinations were tested only where they matter: Q742 + Q746 (stage 2) and Q743 + Q746 (stage 3); both leave nothing.
- Repeat orders (returning patients) were not tested; they need an account.
- This report covers Dr Fox only.
- Two-consultation baskets (spray plus finasteride) were followed only to the confirm page; whether the doctor or pharmacy blocks them after payment is unknown.


---

# Hair + Me (UK) — digest
Slug: hair-and-me · Verified: yes · Audiences: men, women

Model: Subscription telehealth: one online questionnaire, review by pharmacist independent prescribers, then a personalised compounded hair-loss medicine (scalp drops or a once-a-day capsule) posted every four weeks at one flat price.
Account wall: Step 'yd', 'Personalise your account' (legal first name, legal last name, preferred name), reached by pressing 'Continue to checkout' on the shopping bag. Everything before it works without an account: all medical questions, date of birth, the UK tick and the bag. The site silently creates an anonymous visitor id on arrival (POST /new-user) and saves each answer to it as you go.
Tested by: live-walk, site-code, public-docs
Coverage: Stage 1 mapped both routes to the account wall. Stage 2 ran 47 replays: 2 baseline replays, 39 one-change experiments and 6 group tests of non-medical answers, each compared with a pool of baseline visits because the server varies screens at random. Covered one change at a time: first tile, age (17 years 11 months, 18, 85), pregnancy, three men's and three women's hair-loss pictures, recent onset, scalp infection and eczema, blood pressure (and its follow-up), eight further men's conditions, PCOS and heart disease for women, postpartum, facial hair, two medicine options (antidepressants; Other + free text), minoxidil allergy, finasteride allergy, previous finasteride, drops versus capsules on both routes, declining finasteride / dutasteride, pets, and a second goal. Covered in group tests: gender identity (six options), hair type, styling, lifestyle statements, the other hair-loss pictures and timings, skin tone, scalp condition, six-month wishes, washing, hiding hair loss, hard water, buying stage and 'what influenced you'. Everything after the legal-name screen (account, photos, payment, the prescriber's choice of formula, check-ins) was not tested and is described from public documents only. Stage 3 (verify): 21 more replays (2 baselines, 17 one-change, 2 medical group tests), all one at a time; the stop, blood-pressure, pregnancy, gender-identity, pets, previous-product and promo rules were re-tested live (two goals, no hair loss and the 'Other' free-text boxes were not), and the 16 formulas and the price were re-checked against the live leaflets.

Questions: 98 (86 before sign-in; 24 branch). Flows: men (47q), women (51q)

## Engine summary
Hair + Me's public consultation collects answers; it does not decide anything a visitor can see. Stage 2 replayed it 47 times (2 baseline replays, 39 experiments that changed one answer, and 6 group tests of non-medical answers) and compared every screen and the bag with a pool of baseline visits. Only a handful of answers do anything visible: sex at birth swaps the men's and women's question blocks; an age under 18 is the single hard stop; two or more goals, a blood-pressure diagnosis and 'pregnant, breastfeeding or likely to conceive' each add one screen; 'no hair loss' skips one; pets add a warning and a 'Better for pet owners' badge on The Capsules; previous treatments and 'Other' reveal a tick-box or a text box; and a gender identity other than man removes one marketing screen from the men's route. Everything else, including patchy or sudden loss, an active scalp infection, heart disease, phaeochromocytoma, depression, prostate or breast cancer history, a minoxidil allergy, choosing drops over capsules and declining finasteride, leads to exactly the same screens and the same bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99. The real engine is a pharmacist prescriber who, after account creation and payment, picks one of 16 compounded formulas (6 capsules, 10 drops). Its published limits are the leaflet contraindications: no finasteride or dutasteride for women; no oral minoxidil with heart disease, phaeochromocytoma or uncontrolled blood pressure; no finasteride or dutasteride with prostate, breast, sexual-function or mental-health history; no drops on an inflamed or infected scalp; only niacinamide drops in pregnancy or breastfeeding. The company says the choice 'isn't automated or AI': a clinician reads the medical history, hair-loss pattern, lifestyle and goals and selects 'the right active ingredients and concentrations'. Its articles call finasteride first-line and dutasteride second-line (more advanced loss, marked crown thinning, or a limited response to finasteride), and point women with PCOS towards once-a-day minoxidil drops. Which strength a patient starts on is not published, and no prescription was seen.

## Rules
| id | when | then | effect | evidence | conf |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | pds-sex = 'Male' (sex assigned at birth) | The men's route is served: 'Ingredients that see results', lifestyle statements, men's hair-loss pictures, the dutasteride / finasteride acknowledgement, men's medicines and conditions lists, and the 'You're not alone' screen. Finasteride and dutasteride formulas are only in play on this route. [The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 2.5 mg; The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 5 mg; The Capsules: dutasteride 0.5 mg + minoxidil 1.25 mg; The Drops: minoxidil 5% + finasteride 0.1%, twice a day; The Drops: minoxidil 5% + tretinoin 0.01% + finasteride 0.1%, once a day; The Drops: finasteride 0.2%, once a day] | extra-questions | observed | high |
| R2 | pds-sex = 'Female' (sex assigned at birth) | The women's route is served: wash frequency, hiding hair loss, hard water, women's hair-loss pictures, pregnancy / breastfeeding, postpartum / facial hair, women's (shorter) medicines and conditions lists. The dutasteride / finasteride acknowledgement is never shown. [The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 2.5 mg; The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 5 mg; The Capsules: dutasteride 0.5 mg + minoxidil 1.25 mg; The Drops: minoxidil 5% + finasteride 0.1%, twice a day; The Drops: minoxidil 5% + tretinoin 0.01% + finasteride 0.1%, once a day; The Drops: finasteride 0.2%, once a day] | extra-questions | observed | high |

The tile does not route anyone: with sex Male the men's questions and the same bag ('Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99, free shipping) follow; with 'Treatment for men' + Female the women's questions follow. | none | observed | high |

The person is not turned away and reaches the same bag ('Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99, free shipping): someone with no hair loss is still sold a 'Prescription' line. | skip-questions | observed | high |
| R8 | mcm / mcf: 'High blood pressure (hypertension) or low blood pressure (hypotension)' ticked | One extra screen, 'How would you describe your blood pressure over the past 12 months?' (Well-controlled / Often elevated / Often low + optional reading). No other condition adds a screen. [The Capsules: minoxidil 0.5 mg; The Capsules: minoxidil 1.25 mg; The Capsules: minoxidil 2.5 mg + vitamin B6 1.4 mg; The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 2.5 mg; The Capsules: finasteride 1 mg + minoxidil 5 mg; The Capsules: dutasteride 0.5 mg + minoxidil 1.25 mg] | extra-questions | observed | high |
| R9 | bp = 'Often elevated' (or 'Often low') | Nothing visible: no warning, no stop, the reading stays optional, and the same bag ('Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99, free shipping) follows. Docs: the oral leaflet rules out minoxidil capsules with uncontrolled hypertension, so the decision is left to the prescriber. (Stage 3: effect type changed from 'flag-for-clinician' to 'none'. Nothing visible changes in the questionnaire itself. ## Who gets what
- Baseline man: 32, hairline loss for over a year, no medical issues, prefers The Capsules, accepts finasteride / dutasteride → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. Seen in runs/baseline-men and runs/s2-base-men-1. Behind the wall (inferred from the leaflets, the site's 'minoxidil + finasteride capsules'
- Baseline woman: 38, widening middle parting for over a year, not pregnant or breastfeeding, no medical issues, prefers The Capsules → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. Identical to the man's bag (runs/baseline-women, runs/s2-base-women-1). Behind the wall (inferred from the leaflets): a minoxidil-only capsu
- Anyone under 18 (tested: man aged 17 years 11 months; woman aged 16) → nothing [observed] Nothing: stop screen 'We're unable to treat you at this time... we're unable to treat your age group.' No bag (runs/s2-m-age-17, runs/scout-women-1). The line sits at the 18th birthday (runs/s2-m-age-18 passed at 18 years and 8 days).
- Woman who answers Yes to 'pregnant, breastfeeding or likely to conceive' (otherwise baseline, capsules preferred) → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. One extra consent screen first: "We can prescribe you a treatment that's considered safe during this time" (runs/s2-w-pg-yes). Docs (pregnan
- Man who declines finasteride / dutasteride on the acknowledgement screen (otherwise baseline) → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. No visible difference from the baseline (runs/s2-m-pp-decline). By the option's own wording (docs) the prescriber is limited to minoxidil-on
- Man or woman who prefers The Drops (otherwise baseline) → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. Same line as for capsules, including '12ml', although the leaflet says the drops come as 28 ml or 56 ml (runs/s2-m-tt-drops, runs/s2-w-tt-dr
- Man with a leaflet contraindication ticked alone: heart disease, phaeochromocytoma, 'often elevated' blood pressure, liver disease, depression / anxiety, sexual dysfunction, prostate or breast cancer history, BPH, antidepressants, minoxidil allergy, finasteride allergy, active scalp infection or scalp eczema / psoriasis → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. No warning, no stop and no change for any of these (14 one-change replays, for example runs/s2-m-mcm-heart, runs/s2-m-mcm-pheo, runs/s2-m-al
- Man or woman with a hair-loss picture that is not pattern hair loss: patches of complete loss, eyebrow / sideburn / hairline loss, sudden recent loss, or no hair loss at all → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. No advice, no photo request and no stop (runs/s2-m-htam-patches, runs/s2-w-htaf-patches, runs/s2-w-htaf-eyebrows, runs/s2-m-htc-recent, runs
- Man aged 85 (otherwise baseline, capsules preferred) → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Bag: 'Prescription · Personalised treatment · 4 week supply · 12ml', £34.99 every four weeks, free shipping, promo-code box. No drug, strength or format named. No upper age limit, extra question or warning (runs/s2-m-age-85).
- Any visitor arriving from a product page (promo code HGSHOPCAPS or HGSHOPDROPS in the URL) → Personalised treatment (the subscription as it appears in the shopping bag) [observed] Same bag line at £14.99 for each of the first three months, then £34.99 (runs/entry-promo-caps-men, stage 1). The questions, including the format preference, are unchanged.

## Strengths
- Very low friction: about 3 minutes, around 30 short screens, no sign-in until after the bag, one flat price (£34.99 per four weeks) whatever the formula, free delivery.
- Never says no before the wall: apart from under-18s, nobody meets a stop screen, so almost every visitor reaches checkout (commercially strong, clinically thin).
- Broad compounded formulary at one price: oral minoxidil in four strengths, finasteride or dutasteride combined with minoxidil in one capsule, topical finasteride, minoxidil + tretinoin, and non-hormonal niacinamide / urea / melatonin drops.
- A route for women who are pregnant, breastfeeding or trying to conceive (niacinamide-based drops) instead of a refusal, with an informed-consent screen and a dedicated leaflet.
- Alcohol-free, water-based minoxidil drops with essential oils (the leaflet labels them 'PGF', usually meaning propylene-glycol-free): an answer to the commonest complaint about high-street minoxidil.
- Clear, well-written informed-consent screen for finasteride / dutasteride (fertility, mood, sexual effects, persistent symptoms) with an explicit opt-out, shown before the medical history.
- Credible clinical front: two consultant dermatologists, named GPhC-registered pharmacist prescribers, published patient leaflets, own pharmacy, one patient record across the +Me brands.
- Pet-safety question, warning and 'Better for pet owners' badge (minoxidil is toxic to cats and dogs): rare in this market and a neat nudge towards the capsule.
- Built-in outcome tracking: two 0 to 10 wellbeing scores at sign-up and monthly progress photos in the account.
- Constant experimentation: the server varies screens, wording and control types between visits, so the funnel is being optimised all the time.

## Weaknesses
- No recommendation before paying: the bag never says which drug, strength or even format the patient will get. Every adult sees the same 'Personalised treatment' line, so 'personalised' is invisible at the moment of purchase.
- No safety feedback in the questionnaire: heart disease, phaeochromocytoma, 'often elevated' blood pressure, an active scalp infection, a minoxidil allergy and depression were each ticked alone and none produced a warning, a stop or a changed offer. The person is walked to checkout and the problem is left to the prescriber after payment.
- No triage: patches of complete hair loss, eyebrow and hairline loss (a scarring pattern), sudden recent shedding and 'Other/unsure' get no advice, no photo request and no GP signposting, although the company's own blog tells people with patchy or sudden loss to 'speak with a medical professional'. Even 'no hair loss' reaches a 'Prescription' bag.
- Question order works against safety: the drops-or-capsules preference is asked before pregnancy, heart, blood-pressure and scalp answers that decide which format is allowed, and is never revisited. A pregnant woman can pick capsules and is not told they are ruled out.
- Thin medical history: no question on thyroid disease, anaemia or iron, kidney disease, family history of hair loss, smoking, alcohol or weight; the men's list has no question on fertility plans beyond the consent text; medicines are three tick-boxes plus free text.
- Thin women's pathway: one combined pregnancy / breastfeeding / 'likely to conceive' question, nothing on contraception (needed for tretinoin), periods, menopause or HRT, and PCOS gets no follow-up. There is no anti-androgen (spironolactone is listed only as a past treatment).
- Single-choice hair-loss picture: a man cannot record hairline and crown together, and there is no severity scale beyond 'moderate / extensive' for diffuse loss.
- Previous treatment is only a tick-list: no question on how long it was used, whether it worked or what side effects it caused, so past finasteride side effects are not captured unless typed elsewhere.
- Photos are optional and asked for after payment, so most diagnoses are made from tick-boxes ('diagnoses at a distance', in the company's words).
- Format preference is not binding and reviewers report being sent pills after asking for a liquid; with no drug named in the bag, the first time a patient learns the formula is on the label.
- No licensed products, shampoo, device or blood test, and capsules and drops cannot be combined (FAQ), so the only step-up is a different compounded formula.
- Pricing surprises: £34.99 headline, £14.99 or £12.99 introductory offers depending on how you arrive, bundles that cannot be cancelled mid-term; reviewers complain about the jump to full price and about losing an older price after a new consultation.
- Loose ends on the site: the bag says '4 week supply · 12ml' even for capsule choosers while the leaflet says 28 ml or 56 ml; one home-page button links to an unfilled placeholder (/HAIR_SIGNUP_URL).
- The marketing describes a fuller assessment than the questionnaire delivers: the female-pattern article says patients are asked for 'clear photographs of your scalp from specific angles' and 'menstrual history and any recent hormonal changes', but no period or menopause question is asked before the account wall and the photo FAQ calls photos 'entirely optional'.
- All the clinical work is done by people after payment ('This isn't automated or AI'), so the service cannot show a plan instantly, and consistency depends on each prescriber reading tick-boxes with no on-screen rules.

## Opportunities
- Show the plan before the account wall: the likely medicine, strength and format, why it was chosen from the answers, and what was ruled out. Hair + Me shows one generic line to everyone, so a visible, explained recommendation is an immediate point of difference.
- React to answers on screen. When someone ticks heart disease, uncontrolled blood pressure, a scalp infection or a minoxidil allergy, say at once what that means ('tablets are not suitable for you; here is what is') instead of staying silent until after payment.
- Do real triage and be proud of it: route patchy, scarring-type or sudden loss to photos, a clinician call or a GP letter before any payment, and tell people with no hair loss that they do not need a prescription.
- Ask the format question last, after the safety answers, and only offer formats the person can actually have.
- Build a proper women's pathway: separate pregnancy, breastfeeding and trying-to-conceive questions, contraception, cycle, menopause and HRT, PCOS detail, iron and thyroid history, with treatments Hair + Me lacks for women (for example spironolactone where appropriate, or blood-test-guided plans).
- Take a real history of past treatment (what, how long, response, side effects) and use it: past finasteride side effects should change the plan automatically.
- Let people record more than one area of loss, severity and family history, and make photos part of the standard assessment with visible progress tracking.
- Offer combination and step-up care (oral plus topical, medicated shampoo, supplements matched to blood results, licensed options alongside compounded ones) where Hair + Me allows one product only.
- Transparent pricing for every plan length before sign-up, the same price however you arrive, and no trial-to-full-price shock.
- Publish the dose ladder (for example starting and maximum oral minoxidil doses for men and women, and when dutasteride is used): Hair + Me lists its formulas but never says who starts on what.
- Ask the questions Hair + Me's own articles say matter but its questionnaire skips: family history, thyroid and iron (with an optional blood test), periods and menopause, and whether earlier finasteride or minoxidil worked. Each can drive a visible rule.
- Encode the safety rules in software so the plan appears at once and a clinician confirms it. Hair + Me relies on a person reading every questionnaire after payment, which is slower and invisible to the buyer.

## Verification verdict
For the part anyone can see, this report is trustworthy. Two fresh baselines and 19 more replays in stage 3 reproduced every visible rule that was re-tested: the only stop is age under 18, a few answers add or skip one screen or add a badge, the promo links lower the first price, and the bag is always the same unnamed £34.99 'Personalised treatment'. No claim was contradicted. The corrections were about labels: a few rules and one option said 'observed' for something no replay can see (a prescriber's decision, a product removal or switch), and these now say 'none', 'docs' or 'inferred'. The product catalogue matches the live leaflets. The weak part is the real decision, which a pharmacist prescriber makes after sign-up and payment: what is said about who gets which formula comes from the company's leaflets and articles and from reasoning, so treat it as likely, not proven.

## Limitations
- The prescriber's decision rules (which answers lead to which of the 16 formulas, which strength, when someone is refused) sit behind the account wall and were not tested. Rules labelled 'docs' quote the company's leaflets, FAQ and terms; they show what must not be prescribed, not what is. No prescription was ever seen.
- 'No visible change' means: same screens, same wording and options, same bag JSON as a pool of baseline visits. An answer can still change the prescriber's choice later.
- The server varies the flow at random between visits (see serverRandomness). Each comparison was made against all baseline visits of that route (3 for men, 4 for women) and against every unrelated run, but the pools are small, so a rare random variant could still be mistaken for an effect or the reverse.
- Budget: 47 stage 2 replays (about 45 were allowed). Not tested as a one-change replay: most women's conditions and medicines (PCOS and heart only), women's allergies, most allergies for men (minoxidil and finasteride only), most scalp symptoms (infection and eczema only), most previous products (finasteride tablets only), BPH medicine, scalp steroids, hypertrichosis, PoTS, 'Often low' blood pressure, 'Complete hair loss' for men, 'receding hairline' and 'I don't know what kind of hair loss I have' for women (never chosen), the women's under-18 stop (seen at 16 in a multi-change scout), ages above 85, a non-UK address (the UK box is a required tick), the allergy free-text box, and the drops promo link. Those that were covered rely on stage 1 multi-change scouts or on the other route, and are labelled so option by option. The answer files prepared for the untested ones are in runs/_not-run/.
- Non-medical answers (hair type, styling, skin tone, wishes, washing, gender identity and so on) were tested in six group tests, 5 to 11 answers at a time. A group test with no visible change shows that none of its answers has a visible effect by itself, unless two effects cancel out, which is unlikely here; it is weaker evidence than a one-change replay. The gender-identity options 'Other', 'Woman/Girl' with sex Male and 'Man/Boy' with sex Female were never chosen.
- Free-text boxes were given one harmless value each ('Vitamin D' for medicines in stage 2; 'Penicillin' for allergies and 'Levothyroxine' for the women's medicines in stage 1 scouts). Whether particular typed words trigger anything was not explored.
- Statements about first-line and second-line drugs, PCOS and further tests come from the company's general explainer articles. They describe how its clinicians think; they are not a published prescribing protocol, and they are labelled low confidence.
- The served code contains screens that were never shown in any walk (a treatment selector with 'Alternatively, choose your preferred ingredients:', a bundle selector, an add-on selector for the vitamins). The code is shared with the other +Me brands, so where and whether Hair + Me uses them is unknown.
- Prices: only the £34.99 headline, the £14.99 product-page offer and the £12.99 referral offer were seen. Bundle prices, the vitamin add-on price and what a patient pays after a dose change are not public; review quotes about prices are third-party reports.
- Customer numbers, awards, review scores and review quotes are the company's or third parties' claims and were not verified.
- Each replay created an anonymous visitor record with fictional answers on Hair + Me's servers (that is how the site works for every visitor). No name, email, phone number, address, photo or payment detail was ever entered and no consultation was submitted for review.


---

# Happy Head (US) — digest
Slug: happy-head · Verified: yes · Audiences: men, women

Model: US dermatologist-founded telehealth company selling custom-compounded prescription hair-loss medicines (topical, oral and combined) on subscription, plus non-prescription hair products.
Account wall: An email page ('BLACK FRIDAY SALE, Up to 60% Off, Plus FREE Dermaroller' on 23 Nov 2026) with a consent tick. Its fields and place depend on a split test chosen when the quiz loads: (a) sms_capture: email + US mobile number, after every medical question (about 26 screens in; runs/baseline-men, runs/verify-base-women-pinned); (b) no redirect: email only, same place at the end (runs/s2/baseline-men, runs/verify-base-men); (c) early_capture: email only, straight after 'Take Me to My Plan', BEFORE the recommendation screen and before any medical question (runs/verify-base-women, runs/s2/var-early-capture-women). Submitting it posts the questionnaire to api.happyhead.com/middleware/embeddables and leads to checkout at my.happyhead.com. Nothing was entered.
Tested by: live-walk, site-code, public-docs
Coverage: Tested live: the standard assessment on www.form.happyhead.com (its own robots.txt allows all agents) for the baseline man and the baseline woman, both to the email page. Read from code: the full quiz definition (62 pages, all conditions, the recommendation call, the order payload with its clinician flags), served to the anonymous page by engine.embeddables.com. Read from documents: 24 help-centre articles and four independent reviews. Not tested: product pages and prices on www.happyhead.com (closed to this agent; a few facts were checked through web-search summaries only), anything after the email page, and what the dermatologist actually does with a flagged answer. Stage 2: 21 one-change experiments and both baselines replayed live on 23 Nov 2026, all stopping at the email page with no consultation posted. Stage 3 (independent check, 23 Nov 2026): both baselines and 12 high-impact rules replayed live in runs/verify-*, plus 4 new one-change tests (woman 50+ choosing Oral, woman with no birth control, woman with household pregnancy, man with low blood pressure). All runs: 0 consultation posts.

Questions: 48 (44 before sign-in; 22 branch). Flows: main (38q), legacy (6q), consult (4q)

## Engine summary
A three-input engine. Only sex, the woman's age band (under 50 or 50+) and the topical/oral preference reach the recommendation call, which runs before any medical question. Men and women 50+ get dutasteride + minoxidil; women under 50 get minoxidil topical + a spironolactone/minoxidil capsule. The topical + capsule "Dual" bundle is always first and pre-selected; the second card is Topical (or SuperCapsule if the person said Oral). After the choice, about 12 to 16 medical questions follow. They add follow-up pages (free text, reactions, partner-pregnancy consent, three blood-pressure questions for capsule plans) but never remove, switch or block a product: 25 live one-change tests (21 in stage 2, 4 more in the stage 3 check) changed the offer only through sex/age band and method. Answers are tagged important or critical for a dermatologist who reviews after checkout. The only hard stop is age under 18, and a woman whose birth date does not fit her band is sent back to choose again. The email wall is a split test: in one arm it comes before the recommendation screen.

| Ultimate Growth Bundle: Topical Solution + SuperCapsule, finasteride & minoxidil | bundle | finasteride unknown; minoxidil unknown | topical solution + daily capsule | both | US prescription, compounded (not FDA-approved as a finished medicine) | unknown | Never offered by the anonymous assessment in any answer combination tested (inferred: sold from its own product page, which was not opened, or chosen by the dermatologist after checkout). | docs |

## Who gets what
- Man, 32, hairline + top, 1-3 years, family history yes, no method preference, no medical issues → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; Topical Dutasteride & Minoxidil (Max Strength) — 'TopicalRx Solution' (formula code TD9) [observed] Heading 'We Recommend Dutasteride + Minoxidil Rx'. Bundle card badged FASTEST RESULTS and '7/10 Customers Choose This Plan'. The service also returned the SuperCapsule alone (p2) but the page hides that card unless the method answer is Oral. No price on the screen. Replayed 23 Nov 2026 (runs/s2/base
- Woman, 38, parting + all over, 1-3 years, able to become pregnant, on the pill, no medical issues → Dual Treatment Plan (women under 50): Custom Topical minoxidil + SuperCapsule spironolactone & minoxidil; Custom Topical: Minoxidil & Retinoic Acid (formula code TM3) [observed] Heading 'We Recommend Minoxidil & Retinoic Acid Rx'. Active-ingredient panel lists spironolactone first. The service also returned the spironolactone SuperCapsule alone (p5), hidden unless the method answer is Oral. Replayed (runs/s2/base-women): same result; $59 + $89 = $148 per 25 days list.
- Man, baseline, method = Oral → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; SuperCapsule: Dutasteride, Minoxidil & Vitamin D3 (formula code SC2) [observed] Dual pre-selected; second card SuperCapsule (dutasteride 0.5 mg, minoxidil 1.25 mg, D3). runs/s2/m-method-oral
- Man, baseline, method = Topical → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; Topical Dutasteride & Minoxidil (Max Strength) — 'TopicalRx Solution' (formula code TD9) [observed] Same as baseline. runs/s2/m-method-topical
- Woman under 50, method = Oral → Dual Treatment Plan (women under 50): Custom Topical minoxidil + SuperCapsule spironolactone & minoxidil; SuperCapsule: Spironolactone, Minoxidil & Vitamin D3 (formula code SC3) [observed] Second card spironolactone SuperCapsule. runs/s2/w-method-oral
- Woman under 50, method = Topical → Dual Treatment Plan (women under 50): Custom Topical minoxidil + SuperCapsule spironolactone & minoxidil; Custom Topical: Minoxidil & Retinoic Acid (formula code TM3) [observed] Same as baseline woman. runs/s2/w-method-topical
- Woman 50 or older (any method answer tested: not sure) → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; Topical Dutasteride & Minoxidil (Max Strength) — 'TopicalRx Solution' (formula code TD9) [observed] Gets the men's dutasteride set, even when she says she can become pregnant. runs/s2/w-50plus, w-50plus-full
- Man who picks the Topical card → Topical Dutasteride & Minoxidil (Max Strength) — 'TopicalRx Solution' (formula code TD9) [observed] $89 per 25 days; three blood-pressure/check-up/weight questions skipped. runs/s2/m-choose-topical
- Man (method Oral) who picks the SuperCapsule card → SuperCapsule: Dutasteride, Minoxidil & Vitamin D3 (formula code SC2) [observed] $89 per 25 days. runs/s2/m-oral-choose-capsule
- Anyone with a date of birth under 18 → nothing [observed] Stopped on the date-of-birth page ("You must be over 18"). runs/s2/m-dob-under18
- Man with red flags (bald patches, scalp symptoms, heart condition, reaction to finasteride, pregnant partner who declines the risk, age 70, low blood pressure) → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; Topical Dutasteride & Minoxidil (Max Strength) — 'TopicalRx Solution' (formula code TD9) [observed] Identical to the baseline man in every case tested; flags go only to the dermatologist after checkout.
- Woman 50 or older, method = Oral → Dual Treatment Plan (men / women 50+): TopicalRx Solution + SuperCapsule, dutasteride & minoxidil; SuperCapsule: Dutasteride, Minoxidil & Vitamin D3 (formula code SC2) [observed] Dutasteride set; capsule card second. runs/verify-w-50plus-oral

## Strengths
- Very short path to a named plan: six profile questions, then a clear two-card choice with a "best for" line on each.
- Leads with dutasteride and 8% minoxidil, stronger than most rivals' first offer, and explains each ingredient.
- A clear age rule for women: no finasteride or dutasteride for women who say they are under 50, and a date-of-birth check that sends a mismatched woman back.
- Branching follow-ups capture detail where it matters (free text for symptoms, conditions, other patterns, reactions; a written partner-pregnancy consent).
- Asks blood pressure, weight and last check-up only when an oral capsule is chosen, keeping the topical path short.
- A customisation box lets the patient ask the doctor to change the formula.

## Weaknesses
- The product is chosen before any safety question, and the most intensive plan (dutasteride + oral minoxidil) is pre-selected for every man, even "just exploring" or "prevention only".
- Red flags do nothing on screen: bald patches, severe scalp symptoms ("may disqualify you"), heart disease with oral minoxidil, a reaction to finasteride, all leave the plan unchanged.
- A pregnant partner who declines finasteride/dutasteride still has dutasteride in the plan; a woman 50+ who can become pregnant is offered dutasteride with no warning.
- Goal, duration, family history and hair-loss pattern are asked "to personalise" but change nothing.
- Age comes from a self-chosen band; the real birth date is asked only at the end and does not change the product. No upper age limit.
- No price on the recommendation screen; price appears only after email and mobile number.
- Finasteride, the usual first-line drug, is never offered by the assessment.

## Opportunities
- Ask safety questions first and let them shape the offer: remove oral minoxidil for heart disease or low blood pressure, drop 5-alpha-reductase inhibitors when the patient declines them, refer patchy loss or scarring signs.
- Use goal, pattern and duration to offer a gentler start (topical only, finasteride) to "prevention" or "exploring" patients.
- Offer finasteride as first line with a plain comparison against dutasteride.
- Show the price on the recommendation screen before asking for contact details.
- Use the actual date of birth (not a band) and handle older adults and post-menopausal women under 50 explicitly.
- Ask about women's specific causes (iron, thyroid, PCOS, recent childbirth or illness), which this quiz does not ask.

## Verification verdict
The core finding holds up well. Every high-impact rule re-tested live (stops, product family by sex and age band, card swap by method, topical-only skipping the blood-pressure questions, medical answers never changing the plan) reproduced exactly, and the served code agrees. The one real error was the account wall: it is a split test, and in one arm it comes before the recommendation is shown. Facts about the six products the quiz returns are reliable (live API data). Facts about the rest of the catalogue are weaker: they rest on help-centre text, third-party reviews and search summaries because the marketing site's robots.txt bars this agent, and one stage 2 price was out of date. Treat the engine logic as high confidence and the wider catalogue prices as rough.

## Limitations
- Product pages on www.happyhead.com were not read (robots.txt closes them to this agent). Prices and strengths for products outside the two baseline recommendations come from the help centre and from third-party reviews of different dates, and may be out of date.
- The recommendation service's own rules are on the server. Only its inputs (sex, a made-up birth date from the age band, method, product ids) and two outputs were seen.
- Nothing after the email page was seen: checkout prices, discounts, photo upload, identity check, and what the dermatologist changes or refuses.
- The flow title contains the word 'Test' and the code holds several price and layout experiments, so screens can differ between visitors and over time.
- State-by-state availability in the US was not found.
- 21 one-change experiments plus the two baselines were run live (runs/s2/). Not tested live: goal "exploring", durations other than 1-3 years, family history "not sure", the other scalp symptoms and health conditions one by one, low/high blood pressure, weight bands, medicines and allergies "Yes", other birth-control answers, "accept" on the partner-pregnancy screen, the women's pattern options other than baseline, and all URL variants (problends, ot, s_p, upgrade, coupon, consult). Their effects come from the served quiz code, which shows none of them change the product.
- The recommendation service is on the server: only its inputs and outputs were seen. A woman 50+ with method Oral was not walked.
- Nothing after the email page (account wall) was seen: what the dermatologist changes, checkout prices and discounts are not observed.
- Stage 3 checked non-quiz products only through web-search summaries; prices there may be promotional and change often.


---

# Hers (US) (US) — digest
Slug: hers-us · Verified: yes · Audiences: women

Model: US telehealth brand for women: a free online hair quiz and intake, review by a licensed provider (no visit), then compounded and off-label prescription hair treatments on subscription, plus over-the-counter minoxidil, supplements and hair care sold directly.
Account wall: Not observed (site blocked). Reported by one reviewer in Dec 2023 (Innerbody, re-read at stage 3): 'You'll have to make a Hers account to see your results', that is, after the quiz and before any recommendation. Later descriptions (a Hims & Hers partner article, May 2025; AOL / New York Post, Jun 2026) say the quiz 'leads to an intake form' and do not say where the account step sits. A Nov 2026 review adds that a government-issued ID and a payment card are needed to complete a consultation (Healthline, about the platform in general). No step behind this wall was tested.
Tested by: public-docs
Coverage: No live walk of the Hers consultation was possible at any of the three stages: the site stays behind a security checkpoint, which was not defeated. No answer was changed and replayed, so nothing in this file is 'observed'. What the file holds is (1) Hers's stated safety and eligibility policy, from patient leaflets in the shared Hims & Hers help centre (opened directly and re-read word for word at stage 3) and from Hers pages read only through a search index; (2) descriptions of the quiz by reviewers and one partner article (2023 to Nov 2026), now labelled 'inferred' because they are not Hers's own statements; (3) one code hint: names of old Hers hair experiments in the page state served by the sister site. These say who Hers says can have each product. Whether the quiz enforces a rule itself (stop screen, product hidden) or leaves it to the provider is not known. Question wording is not verbatim. Hers is women only; men are served by the sister brand Hims.

Questions: 17 (8 before sign-in; 9 branch). Flows: women (17q)

## Engine summary
Untested: the site was blocked at all three stages, so this is how the engine should work according to Hers's own public statements and reviewers, not how it was seen to work. Hers treats one diagnosis, female pattern hair loss, with one main drug, minoxidil, sold in several formats. (1) A short visual quiz collects goals, symptoms, hair density and routine; nothing public shows these changing the product. (2) Health questions, either inside the quiz (2023 report) or in an intake form after it (2025 and 2026 reports), feed three documented gates. Menopause: finasteride (the Postmeno Serum) is only for women past menopause. Pregnancy, planning a pregnancy or breastfeeding: oral minoxidil in any form and finasteride are ruled out. Health: pheochromocytoma rules out oral minoxidil and a heart history must go to the provider (confirmed word for word in the help centre); kidney, liver and blood pressure problems, age under 18 and already using minoxidil are further exclusions read only through a search index. (3) A preference question reported in 2023 (vitamins with your treatment?) probably picks between plain oral minoxidil at $29 a month and the vitamin blends at $35 to $39. (4) One or two prescription products are shown, reportedly after an account is made, and can be swapped; a licensed provider then approves, changes or declines and sets the dose, by message in most states and by video in some. Over-the-counter minoxidil, supplements and hair care sit outside the quiz. Spironolactone may no longer be part of the hair range.

## Rules
| id | when | then | effect | evidence | conf |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| R1 | women-q11 = pre-menopausal (any woman who has not gone through menopause) | Finasteride products are not offered. The offer stays with minoxidil-based products, with spironolactone as a possible alternative. [Hair Blends Postmenopause Serum (Postmeno Serum); Postmenopausal Hair Loss Spray (finasteride + minoxidil)] | product-removed | docs | high |
| R2 | women-q11 = post-menopausal | The finasteride plus minoxidil topical (Hair Blends Postmenopause Serum; earlier a spray) is added to what can be offered, 'for those who qualify'. [Hair Blends Postmenopause Serum (Postmeno Serum); Postmenopausal Hair Loss Spray (finasteride + minoxidil)] | product-added | docs | high |
| R3 | women-q9 = pregnant (or planning a pregnancy) | Oral minoxidil in any form (plain tablets and the minoxidil plus vitamin blends) and the finasteride products are ruled out. Spironolactone would be too (Hers blog via search index, not re-confirmed; the product may no longer be offered). Whether the quiz shows a stop screen or leaves the refusal to the provider is not known. | R4 | women-q9 = breastfeeding | Oral minoxidil products and finasteride are ruled out. Hers says some topical solutions are possible while breastfeeding, so a topical is the likely remaining offer (which one is not stated). [Oral Minoxidil; Biotin + Minoxidil Chew; Biotin + Minoxidil Gummy; Hair Vitamins + Minoxidil; Hair Blends Postmenopause Serum (Postmeno Serum); Postmenopausal Hair Loss Spray (finasteride + minoxidil)] | product-removed | docs | medium |
| R5 | age under 18 | Not eligible for oral minoxidil products through Hers; a search summary of Hers's treatment terms page says such patients are pointed to in-person care. Where the quiz asks age, and whether it stops the whole consultation, is not known. [Oral Minoxidil; Biotin + Minoxidil Chew; Biotin + Minoxidil Gummy; Hair Vitamins + Minoxidil] | product-removed | docs | medium |

Other products are not affected by this rule. [Spironolactone (may no longer be offered for hair loss)] | product-removed | docs | low |

Whether the quiz asks anything that would detect these conditions, or shows a stop screen, is not known. [Oral Minoxidil; Biotin + Minoxidil Chew; Biotin + Minoxidil Gummy; Hair Vitamins + Minoxidil] | product-switched | inferred | low |

The provider sets the oral minoxidil dose: 1.25 mg to start, 2.5 mg possible later. The quiz itself is reported to recommend prescription products only. [Minoxidil 5% Foam; Minoxidil 2% Solution (drops); Biotin Gummies (Biotin Builder); Triple Threat Shampoo and Conditioner; Full Volume (Volumizing) Shampoo and Conditioner; Rapid Repair Hair Mask; Detox Scalp Scrub] | skip-questions | docs | medium |

Which hair treatments are limited, and in which states, is not stated. | product-removed | docs | medium |
